I don't do anything like that. I just... well, I just sit with it, I guess. I don't try to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food happen?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been happening most weeks. Usually just... maybe once or twice. Mostly on those quiet afternoons, you know, after choir practice or when I finish up in the garden. Just... since my friend passed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm not rightly sure. I don't keep a scale in the house anymore. My clothes fit about the same, I suppose. I just... I haven't really paid much mind to it.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food happen?\n\nPatient: Well... like I said, it's... it's most weeks. So I guess... maybe four to eight times a month? It's just... usually once or twice a week, like I mentioned before. On those quiet afternoons.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes where you eat large amounts of comfort food and can't stop yourself are typically followed by any attempts to diet or restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: No... no, I don't think so. I just... I eat what I normally do the rest of the time. I don't really try to diet or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many days in a week do you have one of these episodes where you eat a large amount of comfort food and can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: I... well, I think I mentioned it just a moment ago. It's usually once or twice a week. Most weeks, anyway. Ever since... well, since my friend passed, it's been happening. Just on those quiet afternoons... usually after I get home from choir, or... or when I'm done gardening. Just one or two days.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food and feeling like you can't stop happened at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. Like I said, it's been most weeks... ever since she passed. Just once or twice a week, usually.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you can't stop, but then don't do anything to try to compensate for it, like exercising or fasting?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure what you mean. Usually, if I'm eating a lot like that, it's... it's just like I described before. I don't really do it otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, and afterwards, you don't try to do anything to make up for it, like vomiting, using laxatives, or fasting?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't think so. Like I just said, when I eat like that, it's... it's just the way I told you. I don't do those other things you mentioned. I just... I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or ashamed afterwards?\n\nPatient: No... I'm not really sure what you mean. Usually, if I'm eating a lot like that, it's... it's just like I described before. I don't really do it otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, and afterwards, you don't try to do anything to make up for it, like vomiting, using laxatives, or fasting?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't think so. Like I just said, when I eat like that, it's... it's just the way I told you. I don't do those other things you mentioned. I just... I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or ashamed afterwards?\n\nPatient: No... I always feel awful. Afterward, I just feel so ashamed... and sad. Baking and eating those things, it used to be about family, you know? Taking care of people. Now it's just... something I do alone, something I have to hide from the ladies at church. I feel terrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time and feel like you can't stop, without doing anything to try to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: I... didn't we just go over this? I'm not really sure what else you want me to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large amounts of comfort food?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. I just feel... ashamed, mostly. And sad. Making those foods used to mean family, you know? Caring for people. Now... well, now it's just something I feel like I have to hide from the folks at church.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the extra calories after eating these large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: No... I don't do anything like that. I just... well, I just sit with it, I guess. I don't try to get rid of it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food happen?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been happening most weeks. Usually just... maybe once or twice. Mostly on those quiet afternoons, you know, after choir practice or when I finish up in the garden. Just... since my friend passed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I... well, I'm not rightly sure. I don't keep a scale in the house anymore. My clothes fit about the same, I suppose. I just... I haven't really paid much mind to it.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food happen?\n\nPatient: Well... like I said, it's... it's most weeks. So I guess... maybe four to eight times a month? It's just... usually once or twice a week, like I mentioned before. On those quiet afternoons.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes where you eat large amounts of comfort food and can't stop yourself are typically followed by any attempts to diet or restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: No... no, I don't think so. I just... I eat what I normally do the rest of the time. I don't really try to diet or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many days in a week do you have one of these episodes where you eat a large amount of comfort food and can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: I... well, I think I mentioned it just a moment ago. It's usually once or twice a week. Most weeks, anyway. Ever since... well, since my friend passed, it's been happening. Just on those quiet afternoons... usually after I get home from choir, or... or when I'm done gardening. Just one or two days.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food and feeling like you can't stop happened at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. Like I said, it's been most weeks... ever since she passed. Just once or twice a week, usually.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you can't stop, but then don't do anything to try to compensate for it, like exercising or fasting?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure what you mean. Usually, if I'm eating a lot like that, it's... it's just like I described before. I don't really do it otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, and afterwards, you don't try to do anything to make up for it, like vomiting, using laxatives, or fasting?\n\nPatient: I... no, I don't think so. Like I just said, when I eat like that, it's... it's just the way I told you. I don't do those other things you mentioned. I just... I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling guilty or ashamed afterwards?\n\nPatient: No... I always feel awful. Afterward, I just feel so ashamed... and sad. Baking and eating those things, it used to be about family, you know? Taking care of people. Now it's just... something I do alone, something I have to hide from the ladies at church. I feel terrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time and feel like you can't stop, without doing anything to try to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: I... didn't we just go over this? It's like I said a minute ago. I eat all that food, and I can't seem to stop myself, and then... I just sit there. I don't do anything to make up for it. I'm not really sure what else you want me to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food and feeling like you can't stop yourself more than once a week on average over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose. It's been happening more since my friend passed away. Just... most weeks, really. Usually once or twice. It's mostly in the afternoons, when it's quiet... after choir practice or when I'm done with the gardening. I just... I don't know. Yes.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a month would you say you have one of these episodes where you eat a large amount of comfort food and can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I just said. Since my friend passed, it's been happening most weeks. Usually once or twice. So... I suppose maybe six to eight times a month? I don't keep a tally. It just happens on those quiet afternoons, after choir or the gardening. I'm not sure what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of comfort food?\n\nPatient: No. I... heavens, no. I don't do anything like that. Like I said before, I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after eating a large amount of comfort food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I... I don't take anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight gain since these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food started?\n\nPatient: I... no. I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't really step on a scale much these days, but my clothes still fit about the same. So, no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I feel terrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time and feel like you can't stop, without doing anything to try to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: I... didn't we just go over this? It's like I said a minute ago. I eat all that food, and I can't seem to stop myself, and then... I just sit there. I don't do anything to make up for it. I'm not really sure what else you want me to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food and feeling like you can't stop yourself more than once a week on average over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose. It's been happening more since my friend passed away. Just... most weeks, really. Usually once or twice. It's mostly in the afternoons, when it's quiet... after choir practice or when I'm done with the gardening. I just... I don't know. Yes.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a month would you say you have one of these episodes where you eat a large amount of comfort food and can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: I... well, like I just said. Since my friend passed, it's been happening most weeks. Usually once or twice. So... I suppose maybe six to eight times a month? I don't keep a tally. It just happens on those quiet afternoons, after choir or the gardening. I'm not sure what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of comfort food?\n\nPatient: No. I... heavens, no. I don't do anything like that. Like I said before, I just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after eating a large amount of comfort food?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I... I don't take anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight gain since these episodes of eating large amounts of comfort food started?\n\nPatient: I... no. I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't really step on a scale much these days, but my clothes still fit about the same. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these episodes of eating a large amount of comfort food?\n\nPatient: No... I don't do that. I just... I eat my regular meals. I don't skip meals or anything."
